SFC will be the Accounted Entity and the sole Executing Entity for this Programme.

The project will be implemented over a period of 4 years, with an option to extend for another year if necessary. The project will explore change adaptation solutions for climate resilience and improved economic development by boosting local economies, creating jobs, and improving health outcomes through cleaner air and water. The project will also aim to reduce greenhouse gas emissions and enhance community, social, and environmental benefits.

Climate change is expected to worsen freshwater availability and scarcity in its extreme, particularly through increased droughts and floods. This project aims to address these issues by providing clean energy solutions that can help mitigate the impacts of climate change on communities.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL

The Green Climate Fund (GCF) is a global fund created to support projects and programs in developing countries that aim to mitigate or adapt to climate change. The GCF was established by the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change (UNFCCC) in 2010.

## Overview of GCF Operations

The GCF operates under the guidance of its Board, which is composed of representatives from both developed and developing countries. The Board makes decisions on funding proposals, sets operational policies, and ensures that the fund is managed effectively.

### Funding Allocation

GCF funds are allocated through various channels, including direct access entities (DAEs) and accredited national implementing entities (NIEs). DAEs are private or public institutions that have been approved by the GCF to directly access funding. NIEs are national entities that have been accredited to implement projects funded by the GCF.

### Key Programs

The GCF has several key programs aimed at addressing climate change issues:

- **Mitigation Program**: Focuses on reducing greenhouse gas emissions and promoting sustainable development.
- **Adaptation Program**: Aims to enhance adaptive capacity, strengthen resilience, and reduce vulnerability to climate change impacts.
- **Pilot Program for Climate Resilience (PPCR)**: Supports countries in building resilience to the adverse effects of climate change.

### Funding Process

The funding process involves several steps:

1. **Project Preparation**: Countries or entities develop project proposals that align with GCF priorities.
2. **Accreditation**: Entities seeking funding must be accredited by the GCF.
3. **Submission and Review**: Proposals are submitted to the GCF for review and approval.
4. **Funding Decision**: The GCF Board makes decisions on funding based on the proposals' alignment with GCF priorities.

### Notable Projects

Some notable projects funded by the GCF include:

- **Renewable Energy Projects**: Supporting the transition from fossil fuels to renewable energy sources.
- **Climate-Smart Agriculture**: Enhancing agricultural practices to adapt to climate change.
- **Disaster Risk Reduction**: Implementing measures to reduce the impact of natural disasters.

### Challenges and Solutions

Despite its significant contributions, the GCF faces several challenges:

- **Funding Limitations**: The GCF has faced criticism for not meeting its funding targets.
- **Complex Application Process**: The process for accessing funds can be complex and time-consuming.
- **Capacity Building**: Developing countries often lack the capacity to implement large-scale climate projects.

To address these challenges, the GCF is working on streamlining processes, enhancing capacity building efforts, and increasing engagement with local communities.

## Conclusion

The Green Climate Fund plays a crucial role in global efforts to combat climate change. By providing funding for mitigation and adaptation projects, the GCF helps countries build resilience and promote sustainable development. Despite facing challenges, the GCF continues to be an essential component of international climate finance.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V2.1 PAGE 3 OF 5

## B. PROJECT/PROGRAMME INFORMATION

### 1. Climate change impact: 100 tons, approximately 2 gigatons.

#### 1.1. Project name: Microscale Urban Heat Island (UMHI) mitigation.

#### 1.2. Project location: Urban areas in the North Pacific region.

#### 1.3. Project duration: 5 years.

#### 1.4. Project objective: The objective is to mitigate the urban heat island effect and reduce greenhouse gas emissions by implementing green infrastructure such as green roofs, walls, and pavements. This project aims to create a model for other cities in the region to follow.

### 2. Project description:

The UMHI project will focus on mitigating the urban heat island effect through the implementation of green infrastructure. The project will involve the installation of green roofs, walls, and pavements in urban areas. These measures will help to reduce the urban heat island effect by providing shade and reducing the amount of heat absorbed by buildings and other surfaces.

### 3. Project activities:

- Installation of green roofs on public buildings.
- Installation of green walls on public buildings.
- Installation of green pavements in public spaces.
- Promotion of green infrastructure through community outreach programs.

### 4. Project outcomes:

The project aims to achieve the following outcomes:
- Reduction of greenhouse gas emissions by 2 gigatons.
- Improvement of air quality in urban areas.
- Creation of green jobs and economic opportunities.
- Enhancement of urban biodiversity.

### 5. Project benefits:

#### 5.1. Environmental benefits:

- The UMHI project will contribute to the reduction of greenhouse gas emissions, which will help to mitigate climate change.
- The project will improve air quality in urban areas by reducing pollutants such as nitrogen dioxide and particulate matter.
- The project will create a model for other cities in the region to follow.

#### 5.2. Social benefits:

- The UMHI project will provide green jobs and economic opportunities for local communities.
- The project will enhance urban biodiversity, providing habitat for wildlife and improving the quality of life for residents.
- The project will improve the aesthetic value of urban areas, making them more pleasant places to live and work.

### 6. Project risks:

#### 6.1. Financial risks:

- The UMHI project may face financial challenges due to the high cost of green infrastructure.
- The project may face delays in implementation due to regulatory or permitting issues.

#### 6.2. Technical risks:

- The UMHI project may face technical challenges related to the installation and maintenance of green infrastructure.
- The project may face challenges related to the integration of green infrastructure into existing urban infrastructure.

### Historical context:

- First Report on Urban Heat Island Mitigation Strategies, published by the Intergovernmental Panel on Climate Change (IPCC) in 2014.
- Second Report on Urban Heat Island Mitigation Strategies, published by the IPCC in 2018.

## Sea-level Rise
Between 1990 and 2015, FSM has experienced a disproportionate amount of sea level rise, varying from 8 to 12 mm per year, compared to other areas in the Western Pacific (see Figure 2 below). Since 2006, the average rate of sea-level rise has been higher than the global average. Although the causes are not well understood, it is likely that local factors such as changes in land use and coastal development have contributed to this increase. Additionally, ENSO events result in a mean sea level anomaly which can exacerbate the effects of long-term sea-level rise.

### Observations
- The observed rate of change has been on ENSO events and seasonal variations, but it is clear that ENSO events result in a direct increase in large-scale sea level anomalies (SLA) in the Western Tropical Pacific.

## Pacific-Australia Climate Change Science Assistance Package Activities
The Green Climate Fund (GCF) Readiness Preparation Support (RPS) project aims to build the capacity of the FSM National Designated Authority (NDA), Ministry of Environment, Climate Change and Emergency Management (MECCEM), and Department of Environment and Climate Change (DECC) to effectively manage climate change-related projects.

### Objectives
- To enhance the capacity of the NDA and MECCEM to effectively manage GCF resources.
- To support the development of a robust National Adaptation Plan (NAP) process in FSM.

## Part A: Impacts

### Future risks

Future risks include the amplification effect of climate change during ENSO (El Niño-Southern Oscillation) events, which can exacerbate extreme weather events such as droughts, floods, and storms. These events could damage marine infrastructure, disrupt fisheries, and affect coastal communities. Additionally, increased frequency and intensity of extreme weather events could lead to more frequent and severe storm surges, flooding, and other impacts.

### Figure 2: Regional distribution of extreme sea surface temperature (SST) anomalies during the ENSO events

![Figure 2](image_url)

#### Extreme climate events (ECEs)

- **TSM**: Tightly coupled to extreme weather events such as tropical storms and hurricanes.
- **2015/16 El Niño**: Caused a storm surge that resulted in up to $50 million in direct and indirect damage at the coast of Fiji. The event also caused significant damage to infrastructure, including roads, bridges, and buildings.
- **2018 Typhoon Mangkhut**: Affected 30% of the population in the Philippines, causing $476 million in damages. The typhoon also affected agriculture and fisheries, leading to a loss of $50 million in revenue.
- **2019 Cyclone Idai**: Caused $500 million in damages across three countries (Mozambique, Malawi, and Zimbabwe), affecting 1.85 million people.

### ENSO phenomena

The ENSO phenomenon has a profound effect on the distribution of tropical cyclones in the FSM during La Niña events. During El Niño events, the number of tropical cyclones increases significantly, with an increase of up to 60% in the western Pacific Ocean. This leads to more frequent and intense storms, which can cause significant damage to infrastructure and communities.

## Climate change projections

Climate change projections are used to estimate average global sea level rise between now and 2100 under different emissions scenarios. The IPCC AR5 provides a range of possible outcomes for each scenario, with the high emission, "business as usual" (RCP8.5) scenario resulting in an increase of up to 98 cm by 2100. Table 1 below shows the projected changes in annual average surface air temperature (FSAT).

| FSAT (°C) | 2030 | 2040 | 2050 | 2060 | 2070 | 2080 |
|-----------|------|------|------|------|------|------|
| RCP2.6    | -1.3 | -1.4 | -1.5 | -1.6 | -1.7 | -1.8 |
| RCP4.5    | 0.9  | 1.1  | 1.3  | 1.5  | 1.7  | 1.9  |
| RCP6.0    | 2.1  | 2.4  | 2.7  | 3.0  | 3.3  | 3.6  |
| RCP8.5    | 3.5  | 3.9  | 4.3  | 4.7  | 5.1  | 5.5  |

### 12. Precipitation

Global precipitation is projected to increase by about 7% under a high emission scenario (RCP8.5) by the end of this century, with no decrease in the frequency of droughts over an increase in their intensity.

- Under a high emission scenario, an increase in mean annual precipitation of up to 10% is expected.
- A 15% increase in extreme FSAT is also projected to increase.

### 13. Sea level rise by 2100

Under a very high emission scenario (RCP8.5), the sea level is projected to be in the range of 74 cm to 98 cm above the 1986-2005 average. This range is based on the IPCC AR5 projections, which include contributions from thermal expansion, changes in ice sheet dynamics, and changes in glaciers and small ice caps.

| Emission Scenario | 2030    | 2040    | 2050    | 2060    |
|------------------|---------|---------|---------|---------|
| Very low (RCP2.6)| 8-19 cm | 14-32 cm| 20-43 cm| 24-57 cm|
| Low (RCP4.5)     | 18-36 cm| 24-48 cm| 31-56 cm| 38-69 cm|
| High (RCP8.5)    | 40-63 cm| 50-74 cm| 61-98 cm| 74-119 cm|

* Climate projections for RCP8.5 have been derived from up to 26 GCMs in the CMIP5 database; the exact number is dependent on the variable and time slice considered.
* The IPCC AR5 provides a range of possible outcomes for each scenario, with the high emission "business as usual" (RCP8.5) scenario resulting in an increase of up to 98 cm by 2100.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL v.2 | PAGE 19 OF 13

## Extreme climate events

Extreme climate events are particularly susceptible to induction errors and even long-term trends from precipitation to temperature extremes can be obscured by extreme events. The IPCC suggests that the frequency of extreme weather events will increase due to climate change, leading to further increases in the risk of droughts, heatwaves, wildfires, storms, and other extreme weather events. This is expected to have significant impacts on human health, agriculture, water resources, and ecosystems.

### Drought: Not years

In FSM, droughts are caused by a combination of factors including reduced rainfall, increased evaporation, and decreased soil moisture. The ENSO phenomenon is the primary driver of drought in the Pacific region. During El Niño events, the Pacific Ocean warms, leading to changes in atmospheric pressure patterns that result in reduced rainfall and increased temperatures. This can lead to drought conditions lasting for several years.

### Extreme climate events

Extreme climate events are particularly susceptible to induction errors and even long-term trends from precipitation to temperature extremes can be obscured by extreme events. The IPCC suggests that the frequency of extreme weather events will increase due to climate change, leading to further increases in the risk of droughts, heatwaves, wildfires, storms, and other extreme weather events. This is expected to have significant impacts on human health, agriculture, water resources, and ecosystems.

### Drought: Not years

In FSM, droughts are caused by a combination of factors including reduced rainfall, increased evaporation, and decreased soil moisture. The ENSO phenomenon is the primary driver of drought in the Pacific region. During El Niño events, the Pacific Ocean warms, leading to changes in atmospheric pressure patterns that result in reduced rainfall and increased temperatures. This can lead to drought conditions lasting for several years.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL

## B

### Executive Summary

The GCF Readiness Programme (R-P) is designed to support developing countries in preparing and implementing their Nationally Determined Contributions (NDCs). The programme aims to enhance the capacity of these countries to access and utilize climate finance effectively.

### Introduction

- **Climate Finance Needs**: Developing countries require significant financial resources to address climate change. The Green Climate Fund (GCF) is a key mechanism for providing this support.
- **Readiness Programme**: The R-P focuses on building institutional capacities in developing countries, enabling them to access GCF funding and implement their NDCs.

### Key Objectives

1. **Capacity Building**: Strengthen the capacity of developing countries to prepare and submit climate change projects to the GCF.
2. **Institutional Support**: Provide support to national designated authorities (NDAs) and other relevant institutions in accessing GCF resources.
3. **Policy Dialogue**: Facilitate policy dialogue on climate finance and NDC implementation.

### Implementation

- **Project Preparation**: Assist countries in preparing project proposals that meet GCF standards.
- **Institutional Support**: Provide training and technical assistance to NDAs and other relevant institutions.
- **Policy Engagement**: Engage with policymakers to promote effective use of climate finance.

### Achievements

- **NDC Implementation**: Supported several countries in implementing their NDCs through GCF-funded projects.
- **Institutional Strengthening**: Enhanced the capacity of NDAs and other relevant institutions to access and utilize climate finance effectively.
- **Policy Dialogue**: Facilitated policy dialogue on climate change and climate finance, leading to improved policies and practices.

### Conclusion

The GCF Readiness Programme has been instrumental in supporting developing countries in preparing and implementing their NDCs. By building capacity, providing institutional support, and facilitating policy dialogue, the R-P has enabled countries to access and utilize climate finance effectively, contributing to global efforts to combat climate change.

**Objective**

The primary objective of this proposal is to secure funding from the Green Climate Fund (GCF) for initiatives aimed at mitigating climate change and promoting sustainable development. The proposed projects focus on renewable energy, energy efficiency, and climate-resilient infrastructure.

**Project Components**

1. **Renewable Energy Initiatives**
   - Installation of solar panels in rural areas.
   - Development of wind farms to generate clean electricity.
   - Implementation of hydroelectric power plants.

2. **Energy Efficiency Programs**
   - Retrofitting buildings with energy-efficient materials and appliances.
   - Promoting the use of LED lighting in public spaces.
   - Energy audits for small and medium-sized enterprises (SMEs).

3. **Climate-Resilient Infrastructure**
   - Construction of flood defenses and drainage systems.
   - Development of early warning systems for natural disasters.
   - Enhancement of water management systems.

4. **Capacity Building and Training**
   - Training programs for local communities on climate change adaptation.
   - Workshops for government officials on sustainable development practices.
   - Educational campaigns to raise awareness about climate change.

5. **Monitoring and Evaluation**
   - Establishment of a monitoring framework to track project progress.
   - Regular reporting on key performance indicators (KPIs).
   - Independent evaluations to ensure accountability and transparency.

**Budget Allocation**

- Renewable Energy Initiatives: $2,000,000
- Energy Efficiency Programs: $1,500,000
- Climate-Resilient Infrastructure: $3,000,000
- Capacity Building and Training: $800,000
- Monitoring and Evaluation: $400,000

**Total Budget:** $7,700,000

---

**Implementation Plan**

1. **Phase 1 (Year 1)**
   - Conduct feasibility studies for renewable energy projects.
   - Develop detailed plans for energy efficiency programs.
   - Initiate community engagement for climate-resilient infrastructure.

2. **Phase 2 (Years 2-3)**
   - Begin construction of renewable energy facilities.
   - Implement energy efficiency measures in buildings and public spaces.
   - Start construction of climate-resilient infrastructure.

3. **Phase 3 (Years 4-5)**
   - Complete all construction projects.
   - Launch capacity building and training programs.
   - Initiate monitoring and evaluation activities.

**Expected Outcomes**

- Reduction in greenhouse gas emissions by 20% within five years.
- Increased access to clean energy for rural communities.
- Enhanced resilience of infrastructure to climate-related risks.
- Improved knowledge and skills among local communities regarding climate change adaptation.
- Robust monitoring and reporting systems ensuring transparency and accountability.

## Types of Instrument

- Grants
    - Provided to support projects in developing countries.
    - Can be used for capacity building and technical assistance.
- Concessional Loans
    - Low-interest loans provided by international financial institutions (IFIs).
    - Used for large-scale infrastructure projects.
- Guarantees
    - Provided by IFIs to reduce risks associated with investments.
    - Encourage private sector participation in climate finance.
- Equities
    - Minority stakes in companies or funds.
    - Can be used to support innovative technologies.

## Pacific Environment Climate Fund – PCF

### The PCF is designed to:

- Support projects that contribute to the objectives of the Paris Agreement (2015).
- Provide funding for mitigation and adaptation activities.
- Encourage private sector participation through blended finance arrangements.
- Align with national development priorities and strategies.

### The PCF can be managed by:

- IFIs or other accredited entities.
- Projects must meet the GCF’s environmental and social safeguards (ES Safeguards).
- The GCF Secretariat provides accreditation and oversight to ensure compliance.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUND FUNDING PROPOSAL

## B

### Executive Summary

The Pacific Islands Renewable Energy Investment Fund (PIREIF) is a regional financing facility that provides long-term financing to support renewable energy and energy efficiency projects in Pacific Island countries. PIREIF aims to increase access to affordable, reliable, sustainable, and modern energy for all.

### Assistance Focus Areas

- **Renewable Energy Projects**: Support the development of renewable energy projects such as solar, wind, hydro, and geothermal power.
- **Energy Efficiency Projects**: Promote energy efficiency measures in buildings, industries, and transport sectors to reduce greenhouse gas emissions.
- **Grid Connections**: Facilitate connections to regional grids to enable the sharing of renewable energy between islands.

### Rationale

The region's remote location and small size make it challenging to develop large-scale renewable energy projects. PIREIF addresses this by providing long-term financing, technical assistance, and capacity building support. This enables countries to transition from fossil fuel-based power generation to renewable sources, reducing greenhouse gas emissions and improving energy security.

### R&D Goals through Free Standing Projects

- **Technology Transfer**: Promote the transfer of clean technologies to Pacific Island countries.
- **Knowledge Sharing**: Facilitate knowledge sharing among member countries on best practices in renewable energy and energy efficiency.
- **Capacity Building**: Provide training and capacity building programs for local engineers, technicians, and policymakers.

### Theory of Change Goals, 1000 words, approximately 2 pages plus diagram

#### Strategic Objectives

1. **Information barriers**:
   - Limited scientific and scholarly knowledge and understanding of climate change and adaptation in the Pacific Islands.
   - Lack of access to relevant information and data on renewable energy technologies.

2. **Limited capacity**:
   - Inadequate human resources for planning, implementation, and maintenance of local security, water security, and energy security projects.
   - Limited technical expertise and financial resources to implement renewable energy projects.

3. **Lack of central repository**:
   - No centralized database or repository of climate change information and assessments as needed at the state level.

#### Strategic Actions

1. **Information barriers**:
   - Develop a regional knowledge hub to provide access to scientific and scholarly research on climate change and adaptation.
   - Create an online platform for sharing best practices, case studies, and lessons learned from renewable energy projects in the region.

2. **Limited capacity**:
   - Establish training programs and workshops to build technical expertise and financial resources for implementing renewable energy projects.
   - Provide grants and low-interest loans to support the development of renewable energy projects.

3. **Lack of central repository**:
   - Develop a centralized database or repository of climate change information and assessments to support decision-making at the state level.
   - Facilitate access to relevant data and information on renewable energy technologies and best practices.

### Notes

- * High-level indicators being used
- * FSP: Sectoral National Communication to the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change, Adaptation

### Change Management

Change management entails the priority given to organizational planning and capacity building in countries implementing the GCF-funded projects. The GCF Secretariat will work with accredited entities to ensure that change management is integrated into their project proposals. Countries implementing GCF-funded projects should be supported by the accredited entities to build institutional capacities for effective implementation of climate change adaptation and mitigation projects.

### Limited Expertise in Designing, Implementing and Managing Climate Change Adaptation Projects

Countries have limited expertise in designing, implementing and managing climate change adaptation projects and programs. This is due to the lack of capacity building and training opportunities available with local governments and communities. There are also limited resources for developing and implementing climate change adaptation projects.

### Financial Barriers

Financial barriers are a significant constraint on the ability of countries to implement climate change adaptation projects. Countries often do not have access to sufficient funding to support their climate change adaptation efforts. This is due to the lack of financial resources available to them, as well as the high cost of implementing climate change adaptation projects.

### Institutional Barriers

Institutional barriers are a significant constraint on the ability of countries to implement climate change adaptation projects. Countries often do not have access to sufficient institutional capacity to support their climate change adaptation efforts. This is due to the lack of institutional capacity available to them, as well as the high cost of implementing climate change adaptation projects.

### Traditional Social Structures and Concepts

Traditional social structures and concepts can create complexities for the implementation of certain climate change adaptation projects. Countries often have traditional social structures that are not compatible with modern climate change adaptation practices. This can make it difficult to implement certain climate change adaptation projects, as well as to ensure their effectiveness.

# Green Climate Fund Funding Proposal

## Kansas: Reuse Two Similar Items to Reduce Requiring Land and Improve Water Quality

Kansas Land Court has identified two similar items that can be reused to reduce requiring land and improve water quality. These items are located in the public domain, where they have been used for many years. Any use of these items, lands, and other natural resources must be regulated by the state and the municipality where the marine space is located.

### Objective

The objective is to reuse two similar items that can be reused to reduce requiring land and improve water quality. The Kansas Land Court would like it back to the original design with supported programs from the public. The State Court would take it back to the original design with supported programs from the public.

### Choke

Land ownership and their new laws require more planning, research, and development. Choke the use of these items to reduce requiring land and improve water quality. This is a new way to reuse two similar items that can be reused to reduce requiring land and improve water quality.

### Summary

Standardized and regulated use of natural resources will ensure long-term representation benefits to public land design and water quality. The Kansas Land Court has identified two similar items that can be reused to reduce requiring land and improve water quality. The identification of natural resources, lands, and infrastructure have many positive impacts on the environment. This is a new way to reuse two similar items that can be reused to reduce requiring land and improve water quality.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUND FUNDING PROPOSAL

## Introduction and objectives

The Green Climate Fund (GCF) is a financial mechanism established by the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change (UNFCCC). The GCF supports projects, programmes, policies, and other activities in developing countries aimed at mitigating and adapting to climate change. This proposal outlines the objectives, structure, and key components of the GCF funding initiative.

### Objectives

- **Mitigation**: Reduce greenhouse gas emissions.
- **Adaptation**: Enhance resilience to climate change impacts.
- **Capacity Building**: Strengthen institutional capacities in developing countries.
- **Technology Transfer**: Promote access to environmentally sound technologies.

## Theories of Change (ToC)

The GCF's ToC builds on the principle that reducing the vulnerability of local communities and enhancing their adaptive capacity is essential for sustainable development. This approach aims to achieve long-term climate resilience by addressing both mitigation and adaptation needs. The GCF will focus on the following key areas:

- **Climate Resilience**: Enhance the ability of communities to adapt to climate change impacts.
- **Economic Growth**: Promote sustainable economic growth in developing countries.
- **Environmental Protection**: Support initiatives that protect and restore natural ecosystems.

### Key Components

1. **Funding Mechanism**: The GCF will allocate funds through various channels, including direct access entities and accredited national implementing entities (NIEs).
2. **Project Portfolio**: A diverse portfolio of projects addressing both mitigation and adaptation needs.
3. **Stakeholder Engagement**: Active engagement with stakeholders to ensure transparency and accountability.

### Implementation Strategy

- **Initial Funding**: Secure initial funding from various sources, including government contributions and private sector investments.
- **Project Selection**: Develop a robust process for selecting projects that meet the GCF's criteria.
- **Monitoring and Evaluation**: Establish systems to monitor and evaluate project progress and impact.

## Conclusion

The GCF aims to address the urgent needs of developing countries by providing financial support for climate change mitigation and adaptation. By focusing on resilience, economic growth, and environmental protection, the GCF seeks to enhance sustainable development outcomes. The success of the GCF will depend on effective collaboration with various stakeholders, including governments, private sector entities, and civil society organizations.

---

The results showed that 80% of LAs had procurement capacities below $10 million USD, with about 30% having capacities below $5 million USD. For more information on the survey conducted.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL

## PAGE 2 OF 5

| Project Name | Description | Objective | Risks | Mitigation Strategies |
|--------------|-------------|-----------|-------|-----------------------|
| **Project A** | Provides clean water to rural communities. | Supplies clean drinking water to remote villages, reducing waterborne diseases. | Dependence on rainfall and seasonal variations. | Implement rainwater harvesting systems and develop small-scale irrigation projects. |
| **Project B** | Establishes solar power plants in urban areas. | Reduces reliance on fossil fuels by providing renewable energy. | High initial costs. | Secure funding through grants and carbon credits. |
| **Project C** | Constructs green buildings in cities. | Promotes sustainable architecture, reducing energy consumption. | Dependence on government regulations. | Advocate for green building standards and secure private investments. |

## Financial Analysis

### Project A
- **Initial Investment**: $500,000
- **Expected Return**: $700,000 over 5 years
- **Risk Assessment**: Medium
- **Mitigation Strategies**: Diversify funding sources, implement water conservation measures.

### Project B
- **Initial Investment**: $1,000,000
- **Expected Return**: $1,400,000 over 7 years
- **Risk Assessment**: High
- **Mitigation Strategies**: Secure long-term power purchase agreements, explore tax incentives.

### Project C
- **Initial Investment**: $800,000
- **Expected Return**: $1,200,000 over 6 years
- **Risk Assessment**: Low to Medium
- **Mitigation Strategies**: Partner with green building councils, leverage energy efficiency rebates.

## S.1: Project/Programme Name Description (area: 2023, focus: sustainability & resilience)

"According to the Joint Monitoring Programme (JMP), 'access to safely managed water is defined as the availability of at least 20 liters per person per day from an improved source which includes "piped water on premises", "piped water nearby", and "water well or protected dug well'." "Improved sources" include:

- Piped water into dwelling
- Piped water to plot
- Public tap or standpipe
- Tubewell or borehole

Access to safely managed sanitation is defined as the use of an improved facility which is not shared with other households and where excreta are safely disposed of in situ or transported and treated off-site. Improved facilities include flush/pour-flush to piped sewer system, septic tank, or pit latrine."

## Page 1 of 1

### Figure 2: Summary of climate-related commitments to GCF

#### Component 1: Local authorities committed to deliver climate change adaptation services to their associate GEF SMEs (USD 70,000)

#### Output 1.1: Local authorities are encouraged to deliver climate change adaptation services to their potential beneficiaries.

- **Disbursement:** USD 50,000
- **Timeframe:** 2 years
- **Description:** The project will focus on the delivery of climate change adaptation services to local communities and small businesses. This includes providing training, technical assistance, and financial support for climate change mitigation and adaptation projects.

#### Component 1: Other activities

- **Output 1.2:** Provide local authorities with knowledge management tools for delivering climate change adaptation services.
- **Output 1.3:** Develop a knowledge management framework to support the delivery of climate change adaptation services by local authorities.
- **Output 1.4:** Create a database of best practices and lessons learned from other local authorities that have successfully delivered climate change adaptation services.

### Component 2: Local authorities committed to deliver climate change mitigation services to their associate GEF SMEs (USD 30,000)

#### Output 2.1: Local authorities are encouraged to deliver climate change mitigation services to their potential beneficiaries.

- **Disbursement:** USD 25,000
- **Timeframe:** 2 years
- **Description:** The project will focus on the delivery of climate change mitigation services to local communities and small businesses. This includes providing training, technical assistance, and financial support for climate change mitigation projects.

#### Component 2: Other activities

- **Output 2.2:** Provide local authorities with knowledge management tools for delivering climate change mitigation services.
- **Output 2.3:** Develop a knowledge management framework to support the delivery of climate change mitigation services by local authorities.
- **Output 2.4:** Create a database of best practices and lessons learned from other local authorities that have successfully delivered climate change mitigation services.

### Component 3: Local authorities committed to deliver climate change adaptation and mitigation services to their associate GEF SMEs (USD 50,000)

#### Output 3.1: Local authorities are encouraged to deliver both climate change adaptation and mitigation services to their potential beneficiaries.

- **Disbursement:** USD 40,000
- **Timeframe:** 2 years
- **Description:** The project will focus on the delivery of both climate change adaptation and mitigation services to local communities and small businesses. This includes providing training, technical assistance, and financial support for projects that address both adaptation and mitigation needs.

#### Component 3: Other activities

- **Output 3.2:** Provide local authorities with knowledge management tools for delivering both climate change adaptation and mitigation services.
- **Output 3.3:** Develop a knowledge management framework to support the delivery of both climate change adaptation and mitigation services by local authorities.
- **Output 3.4:** Create a database of best practices and lessons learned from other local authorities that have successfully delivered both climate change adaptation and mitigation services.

### Output 1: Build institutional expertise to identify, prioritize, and support adaptation solutions

#### Activity 1.1: Local authorities are trained in identifying, prioritizing, and supporting adaptation solutions.

- **Disbursement:** USD 20,000
- **Timeframe:** 2 years
- **Description:** The project will focus on training local authorities to identify, prioritize, and support adaptation solutions. This includes providing technical assistance and financial support for projects that address the most pressing climate change adaptation needs.

#### Activity 1.2: Local authorities are trained in identifying, prioritizing, and supporting mitigation solutions.

- **Disbursement:** USD 15,000
- **Timeframe:** 2 years
- **Description:** The project will focus on training local authorities to identify, prioritize, and support mitigation solutions. This includes providing technical assistance and financial support for projects that address the most pressing climate change mitigation needs.

### Output 2: Build institutional expertise to identify, prioritize, and support adaptation and mitigation solutions

#### Activity 2.1: Local authorities are trained in identifying, prioritizing, and supporting both adaptation and mitigation solutions.

- **Disbursement:** USD 30,000
- **Timeframe:** 2 years
- **Description:** The project will focus on training local authorities to identify, prioritize, and support both adaptation and mitigation solutions. This includes providing technical assistance and financial support for projects that address the most pressing climate change needs.

#### Activity 2.2: Local authorities are trained in implementing adaptation and mitigation solutions.

- **Disbursement:** USD 15,000
- **Timeframe:** 2 years
- **Description:** The project will focus on training local authorities to implement both adaptation and mitigation solutions. This includes providing technical assistance and financial support for projects that address the most pressing climate change needs.

Training curriculum required by countries, data and analysis that all relevant LAAs (Local Administrative Authorities) and sectors be trained to ensure that the GCF Readiness Program is implemented effectively.

## Training Curriculum

- **Objective**: To develop a training curriculum for local administrative authorities.
- **Approach**: Focus on existing and recommended tools that will be adopted as data is collected. TFA Application will be used to train all relevant LAAs in the use of these tools.
- **Department Contact**: Department of Environment. Existing tools include the Public Administration Reform Toolkit, the USAID Public Financial Management Toolkit, and the USAID Public Expenditure Management System (PEMS). The USAID Public Financial Management Toolkit will be used to train local administrative authorities.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL

## B

### Methods As well, the EDA Program provides the first specialized Consultants support to Resilient Communities Grant Facility (RCGF) to enhance their capacity in developing and implementing climate change adaptation management plans for disaster risk reduction. Alignment with GCF funding requirements will be ensured through the development of a comprehensive set of guidelines and templates. The first Resilience Unit, which can be fully staffed and operational by January 2023, will be able to grant disbursements as early as February 2023.

### Domestic Tax Incentives

| Type of Incentive | Description |
|-------------------|-------------|
| Income Tax Credit | Provides a tax credit for businesses that invest in renewable energy projects. |
| Property Tax Exemption | Exempts certain green buildings from property taxes. |
| Sales Tax Holiday | Allows for a temporary reduction or elimination of sales tax on energy-efficient products. |

### Affordable Interventions

- **Energy Efficiency**: Promote the use of energy-efficient appliances and lighting.
- **Renewable Energy**: Encourage the adoption of renewable energy sources such as solar and wind power.
- **Green Building Standards**: Implement green building standards for new constructions.
- **Public Transportation**: Improve public transportation systems to reduce carbon emissions.

### Output 1.1 Establishment of Resilient Communities Grant Facility (RCGF)

The RCGF will be established to provide funding support to resilient communities. The facility will focus on community-based disaster risk management and climate change adaptation initiatives. The RCGF will be managed by a Technical Advisory Committee, which includes representatives from relevant government agencies, non-governmental organizations, and private sector entities.

### Output 1.2 Enhancements of Resilient Communities Grant Facility (RCGF)

Enhancements to the RCGF will include the following:

- **Guidelines Development**: Develop comprehensive guidelines for grant applications.
- **Capacity Building**: Provide training and capacity-building programs for grantees.
- **Monitoring and Evaluation**: Implement a robust monitoring and evaluation system to track progress and impact.

### Output 1.3 Establishment of Resilient Communities Fund

The Resilient Communities Fund will be established to provide long-term support to resilient communities. The fund will be managed by a Board of Trustees, which includes representatives from relevant government agencies, non-governmental organizations, and private sector entities.

# Green Climate Fund Funding Proposal

## Activity 1.1: Development through an RCOF-approved project financing partnerships, ECU, INA, France, SGOF, French Government and Department of France and Administration (GOC)

The GOC NDC will involve a committee, review panel, including members of the EAA and private sector experts to ensure that the project is consistent with the objectives of the Paris Agreement. The project will be implemented through a partnership between the GCF and the INA, which includes the following components:

- **Component 1:** Development of a comprehensive plan for the implementation of the project.
- **Component 2:** Establishment of a monitoring system to track progress and ensure compliance with the objectives of the Paris Agreement.

### Activity 1.1.2: Establishment of inclusive and participatory EDF Program Budget and Green Finance

The GOC will establish an inclusive and participatory budget for the EDF program, which will be used to fund projects that are consistent with the objectives of the Paris Agreement. The budget will be developed through a transparent and inclusive process that involves representatives from the private sector, civil society, and other stakeholders.

### Activity 1.2: GCF governance and procedures standards and strategies for implementing partnerships

The GOC will establish the governance structure and procedures for the implementation of the RCOF project. This will include the establishment of a steering committee, which will be responsible for overseeing the implementation of the project and ensuring that it is consistent with the objectives of the Paris Agreement.

### Activity 1.2.1: Development of gender-responsive strategies

The GOC will develop gender-responsive strategies to ensure that the project is inclusive and equitable. This will include the establishment of a gender-responsive budget, which will be used to fund projects that are consistent with the objectives of the Paris Agreement.

### Activity 1.2.1.a: Development of labor standards for the technical support to rural communities

The GOC will develop labor standards for the technical support provided to rural communities as part of the project. This will ensure that the project is implemented in a manner that is consistent with international labor standards and promotes decent work.

### Activity 1.2.1.b: Development of gender-responsive criteria for project selection

The GOC will develop gender-responsive criteria for the selection of projects funded through the RCOF program. This will ensure that the project is inclusive and equitable, and that it promotes gender equality and women's empowerment.

## Output 2: Green climate finance activities

### Output 2.1: Establishment of a green climate fund

The GOC will establish a green climate fund to support the implementation of the RCOF project. This will include the establishment of a trust fund, which will be used to finance projects that are consistent with the objectives of the Paris Agreement.

### Output 2.2: Development of a green climate finance strategy

The GOC will develop a green climate finance strategy to guide the implementation of the RCOF project. This will include the establishment of a green climate finance board, which will be responsible for overseeing the implementation of the strategy and ensuring that it is consistent with the objectives of the Paris Agreement.

### Output 2.3: Development of a green climate finance monitoring system

The GOC will develop a green climate finance monitoring system to track progress towards the objectives of the RCOF project. This will include the establishment of a green climate finance dashboard, which will be used to monitor the implementation of the project and ensure that it is consistent with the objectives of the Paris Agreement.

### Output 2.4: Development of a green climate finance reporting system

The GOC will develop a green climate finance reporting system to report on progress towards the objectives of the RCOF project. This will include the establishment of a green climate finance report, which will be used to communicate the results of the project and ensure that it is consistent with the objectives of the Paris Agreement.

### Output 2.5: Development of a green climate finance communication strategy

The GOC will develop a green climate finance communication strategy to promote the implementation of the RCOF project. This will include the establishment of a green climate finance website, which will be used to communicate the results of the project and ensure that it is consistent with the objectives of the Paris Agreement.

### Output 2.6: Development of a green climate finance capacity building program

The GOC will develop a green climate finance capacity building program to support the implementation of the RCOF project. This will include the establishment of a training program, which will be used to build the capacity of stakeholders to implement the project and ensure that it is consistent with the objectives of the Paris Agreement.

### Output 2.7: Development of a green climate finance impact assessment system

The GOC will develop a green climate finance impact assessment system to assess the impacts of the RCOF project. This will include the establishment of an impact assessment framework, which will be used to evaluate the impacts of the project and ensure that it is consistent with the objectives of the Paris Agreement.

### Output 2.8: Development of a green climate finance risk management system

The GOC will develop a green climate finance risk management system to manage the risks associated with the implementation of the RCOF project. This will include the establishment of a risk management framework, which will be used to identify and mitigate the risks associated with the project and ensure that it is consistent with the objectives of the Paris Agreement.

### Output 2.9: Development of a green climate finance evaluation system

The GOC will develop a green climate finance evaluation system to evaluate the effectiveness of the RCOF project. This will include the establishment of an evaluation framework, which will be used to assess the performance of the project and ensure that it is consistent with the objectives of the Paris Agreement.

### Output 2.10: Development of a green climate finance sustainability system

The GOC will develop a green climate finance sustainability system to promote the long-term sustainability of the RCOF project. This will include the establishment of a sustainability framework, which will be used to ensure that the project is implemented in a manner that is consistent with the objectives of the Paris Agreement and promotes sustainable development.

### Activity 2.2.1 Initial Call for Expression of Interest

SE. The Programme also prioritizes funding for LDCs that are considered the most vulnerable to climate change based on an assessment of vulnerability indices and 2 relative capacity assessment. As such, the ECU will be allocated in a manner that ensures that at least 50% of the total ECU is allocated to LDCs. This includes Small Island Developing States (SIDS) and Least Developed Countries (LDCs). The initial call for expression of interest will be issued in Activity 2.1.

Based on priority eligibility criteria outlined under Activity 1.1.3 and Activity 1.1.5, ECU will base an upper limit of USD 70,000 per project. This amount is intended to cover the expenses of the GCF readiness proposal development process including the preparation of the GCF Readiness Annual Program (RAP) and the associated activities.

### Activity 2.2.2 Closing of Expressions of Interest

SE. The ECU provides will allow the ECU to pre-screen sub-grantees against the GCF readiness criteria, eligibility under the GCF accreditation process, and the overall readiness needs of the country. This is in line with the requirements of the Readiness Annual Program (RAP).

## Page 1 of 3

The GCF/SCF ensures that the financing benefits of the proposal come under the full fold of the climate action results management system (CARS) and the enhanced direct access modalities. The SCF will be implemented in accordance with the guidance issued by the Least Developed Countries Expert Group (LDCEG) on the use of simplified ECF application with pre-identified schools. ### Key Features

| Feature | Description |
|---------|-------------|
| **Project Title** | Green Climate Fund Proposal |
| **Project Duration** | 2021 - 2023 |
| **Funding Source** | Green Climate Fund (GCF) |
| **Implementing Agency** | Ministry of Environment, Forest and Climate Change |

### Project Objectives

- To enhance the capacity of local communities to adapt to climate change.
- To promote sustainable development in rural areas.

### Project Activities

1. **Community Engagement**
   - Conduct awareness campaigns on climate change adaptation.
   - Provide training on sustainable farming practices.

2. **Infrastructure Development**
   - Construct climate-resilient infrastructure such as schools and health centers.
   - Implement early warning systems for natural disasters.

3. **Capacity Building**
   - Train local communities in climate-smart agriculture.
   - Develop local institutions to manage projects effectively.

### Additional Considerations

Additional considerations to be taken by the ECF in the context of the screening of sub-grants will include the following:

- The M&E plan should clearly specify how indicators will be tracked and reported.
- The MC will provide clear guidance on how sub-grantees will report their progress against the project objectives, outputs, and outcomes.

### Activity 2.3: Integration with Subnational LA/UA

The GCF/SCF will integrate an implementation plan under activity 2.3 to demonstrate the capacity of sub-national LAs/UA to implement ECF projects. The LA/UA will be responsible for implementing the project activities and reporting on progress.

#### Option 1: This lower capacity LA (or the one with a monitoring) should be matched with a higher capacity LA in order to ensure that the project is implemented effectively.

- The higher capacity LA will provide technical assistance to the lower capacity LA.
- The higher capacity LA will also provide training and capacity building for the lower capacity LA.

#### Option 2: This lower capacity LA (or the one with a monitoring) should be matched with a higher capacity LA in order to ensure that the project is implemented effectively.

- The higher capacity LA will provide technical assistance to the lower capacity LA.
- The higher capacity LA will also provide training and capacity building for the lower capacity LA.

### 62.
A data collection exercise will be carried out in order to address the lack of information on food security and nutrition. This exercise will include both qualitative and quantitative data gathering due to permitting to government, non-governmental organizations (NGOs) and other stakeholders. The exercise will also include a review of existing studies on food security and nutrition, as well as an assessment of the number of food production households, number of water points, and land and livestock ownership.

### 63.
The Green Climate Fund (GCF) will provide funding for this exercise.

# Green Climate Fund Funding Proposal

## Executing Entity and Other Implementing Partners

### 77. SPC will be the sole Executing Entity for the proposed programs. A number of units, government departments, and non-governmental organizations (NGOs) will be involved in the implementation.

The SPC acts as a national implementing partner under the climate program to GCF and the relevant regional implementing entities (RIEs). The SPC is also a member of the Pacific Islands Forum Secretariat (PIFS), which is a member of the Green Climate Fund (GCF) Network. The SPC will be responsible for the implementation of the programs.

### FSM, Republic of the Marshall Islands, and Tuvalu

The SPC will work with the following countries to implement the programs:

- **FSM**: The FSM will be responsible for the implementation of the programs in their country.
- **Republic of the Marshall Islands**: The RMI will also be involved in the implementation of the programs.
- **Tuvalu**: Tuvalu will participate in the implementation of the programs.

### Base GCF Focal Points

Each State GCF Focal Point will report the ECU and NOA disbursements to the SPC. The SPC will ensure that all financial reports are submitted on time and that all financial transactions are accounted for. The SPC will also ensure that all financial transactions are in accordance with the GCF Financial Procedures.

## Figure 8 Solve presents an overview of the Programme implementation arrangements described.

### EDA Programme Flow (Red Box)

1. **EDA Program Board (PB)**
   - **Chairperson**
     - Responsible for overall governance and oversight.
   - **Secretariat**
     - Provides administrative support to the PB.
   - **Programme Manager**
     - Manages day-to-day operations of the programme.

2. **Core Risk Fund**
   - **Risk Management Unit (RMU)**
     - Manages risk assessment and mitigation.
   - **Internal Audit Unit (IAU)**
     - Conducts internal audits to ensure compliance with policies and procedures.

3. **Programme Implementation Units (PIUs)**
   - **Project Preparation Facility (PPF)**
     - Provides support for project preparation.
   - **Accreditation Management Unit (AMU)**
     - Manages the accreditation process of implementing entities (IEs).

### EDA Programme Flow of Funds

81. The funding flows for the EDA Programme are illustrated in Figure 9. As an Accredited Entity, SPC's CCES Division will receive funds from the Green Climate Fund (GCF) through its accredited entity status. These funds will be transferred to SPC and then flow to the EDA Programme in the form of grants. The EDA Programme will disburse funds to IEs that have been approved by the EDA Program Board, following a rigorous due diligence process. The IEs will use these funds for project implementation.

82. The disbursement of funds is based on standard grant agreements and governed according to SPC's Financial Regulations (2019). The programme will ensure that all financial controls are in place and that the funds are used efficiently and effectively. More Land Tenure and Coconut-Farming Financing (2020). These policies ensure compliance with internal control requirements.

# Green Climate Fund Funding Proposal v2 | Page 8 of 17

![Green Climate Fund Logo](https://example.com/logo.png)

## Overview

### Mission Statement
Green Climate Fund supports developing countries to limit or reduce greenhouse gas emissions and adapt to climate change.

### EDF Interests

- **Climate Resilience**
- **Renewable Energy**

### Strategic Priorities

1. **Climate Resilience**
2. **Renewable Energy**
3. **Sustainable Transport**
4. **Energy Efficiency**

## EDF Programme Board
The GCF will be chaired by the NDA and will include State-level representation as well as national departments that reflect the different themes the RGCF will support. Disaster risk reduction, climate change adaptation, and mitigation are key priorities. The programme board will also include a climate expert from GECCM, NDA, state GCF focal points, and a representative from EDF's technical team.

## Greenhouse Gas Emissions
Under the GCF Programme, communities can implement technologies to reduce greenhouse gas emissions. This includes renewable energy projects, energy efficiency improvements, and sustainable transport initiatives. The programme will be implemented through a combination of grants and loans.

### Key Decision-Making Process
The final decision will be made by the EPS. The selection criteria will be applied for the selection of the Greenhouse Gas Emissions Programme. The selection process will consider the applicant's experience as well as any other relevant factors that may pertain to the EDF Programme.

## EDF Programme Committee (EPC)
The EPC is composed of an EDF Officer Director and representatives from the NDA, GECCM, and state GCF focal points. The EPC will oversee the implementation of the programme, review any decisions or recommendations made by the programme board, and ensure that the programme is implemented in accordance with the objectives set out in the proposal.

### Funding Agreements
Contracts of the agreements will be signed through a formal process. The EDF will provide technical assistance to support the implementation of the programme. The EDF will also provide financial resources to support the programme.

### Task B: Engage with the AE and EEAs

#### 51. The Pacific Community has been involved in development and environmental protection in the Pacific region for over 70 years.

- GCF is a new funding mechanism that will support climate change adaptation and mitigation projects.
- GCF funds are available to support projects in the Pacific region through accredited entities (AEs) and executing entities (EEAs).

#### 52. SPF Micronesia Regional Office (SPO) has been involved in climate change adaptation and mitigation activities for over 10 years.

- SPO is a regional office of the Secretariat of the Pacific Community (SPC).
- SPO works with national governments to support the development of climate change projects.
- SPO provides technical assistance, capacity building, and project management support to AEs and EEAs in the region.

#### 53. FSM Department of Finance and Administration (DOF) has been involved in GCF funding for over five years.

- DOF is responsible for the development of GCF proposals, including PPFPs and new project concepts.
- DOF works with SPO to develop and submit GCF proposals on behalf of the government.

### 54. The purpose of this proposal is to enhance the engagement of the local climate change community with the GCF.

- The goal is to increase awareness of GCF funding opportunities and to support the development of high-quality project proposals.

## 1.13

The project will both diversify, as well as bolster a set of nationally appropriate DRI, flood and water security measures that are consistent with the objectives of the National Adaptation Plan (NAP) and the Nationally Determined Contribution (NDC). Detailed description of potential technologies and practice principles are presented in Appendix 7 of Annex 2 and 3.

- Soil and water mapping to address water scarcity and promote climate-resilient agriculture (not widely practiced)
- Remotely sensing to address water scarcity (practiced at the household level in some areas of FSM)
- Water harvesting and storage systems (practiced in some areas of FSM; technology is not widely adopted)
- Drip and micro-irrigation to address water scarcity and promote climate-resilient agriculture (not widely practiced)
- Rock reservoirs for DRI (being practiced in some areas of FSM)
- Mangrove restoration and protection by DRI (being practiced in some areas of FSM)

# EXPECTED PERFORMANCE AGAINST INVESTMENT CRITERIA

This section describes the performance of the proposed project against the investment criteria as set out in the GCF’s [RFP](https://www.gcfund.org/rfp).

## 3.1 Expected project outcomes and benefits (1 point)

The proposed EDA Programme contributes to targeted climate resilient livelihood development by improving the adaptive capacity of vulnerable communities through enhanced access to climate information at the local level. It also aims to improve the management and use of States’ FMIs for local climate change adaptation at the community level, thereby enhancing the resilience of vulnerable populations in large settlement areas.

## 3.2 The scale, reach and scope of the subproject is to be supported by the EDA Grant Facility as follows:

- The project will directly benefit 18,000 people (50% women) through improved access to climate information.
- The project will indirectly benefit 97,000 people (50% women) through enhanced adaptive capacity and resilience.

## 3.3 The expected outcomes of the EDA Programme are as follows:

- **Outcome 1:** Enhanced adaptive capacity of vulnerable communities through improved access to climate information at the local level.
- **Outcome 2:** Improved management and use of States’ FMIs for local climate change adaptation at the community level.

## 3.4 The project will be implemented by the following entities:

- The EDA Grant Facility will provide technical assistance and capacity building support to the implementing agencies.
- The implementing agencies will work with local communities to enhance their adaptive capacity and resilience.

## 3.5 The expected benefits of the EDA Programme are as follows:

- **Benefit 1:** Improved access to climate information for vulnerable communities, leading to enhanced adaptive capacity and resilience.
- **Benefit 2:** Improved management and use of States’ FMIs for local climate change adaptation at the community level.

## 3.6 The project will be implemented over a period of three years, with the following milestones:

- **Year 1:** Development of the project design and implementation plan.
- **Year 2:** Implementation of the project activities.
- **Year 3:** Monitoring and evaluation of the project outcomes and benefits.

## 3.7 The expected performance indicators for the EDA Programme are as follows:

- **Indicator 1:** Number of people (by gender) directly benefiting from improved access to climate information.
- **Indicator 2:** Number of people (by gender) indirectly benefiting from enhanced adaptive capacity and resilience.

## 3.8 The project will be implemented in accordance with the following principles:

- **Principle 1:** Gender equality and social inclusion.
- **Principle 2:** Environmental sustainability.
- **Principle 3:** Transparency and accountability.

## 3.9 The project will be monitored and evaluated using the following methods:

- **Method 1:** Regular monitoring of project activities and outcomes by the implementing agencies.
- **Method 2:** Annual evaluation of project performance by an independent evaluator.

## 3.10 The project will be implemented in accordance with the following standards:

- **Standard 1:** Gender equality and social inclusion standards.
- **Standard 2:** Environmental and social safeguards standards.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL

## Introduction to the Proposal

The proposal outlines the need for funding to support projects that address climate change and promote sustainable development.

### Needs of recipient countries

- **Needs of recipient countries:** 600 words, approximately 8 pages.
- **Introduction to the proposal:** 300 words, approximately 4 pages.

## Executive Summary

The executive summary provides an overview of the project, its objectives, and expected outcomes. It highlights the importance of the project in addressing climate change and promoting sustainable development.

### Objectives

- Objective 1: Reduce greenhouse gas emissions by 20% by 2030.
- Objective 2: Increase renewable energy use to 50% by 2030.
- Objective 3: Improve energy efficiency by 30% by 2030.

## Project Description

The project description provides detailed information about the proposed activities, their implementation, and expected outcomes. It includes a timeline for project completion and budget allocation.

### Activities

1. **Activity 1:** Implement renewable energy projects.
   - Sub-activity 1.1: Solar power installation.
   - Sub-activity 1.2: Wind power development.

2. **Activity 2:** Energy efficiency programs.
   - Sub-activity 2.1: LED lighting installation.
   - Sub-activity 2.2: Appliance replacement.

3. **Activity 3:** Capacity building and training.
   - Sub-activity 3.1: Technical training for renewable energy.
   - Sub-activity 3.2: Policy development workshops.

## Budget

The budget outlines the financial resources required to implement the project, including personnel costs, equipment, and other expenses.

### Budget Breakdown

- **Personnel:** $500,000
- **Equipment:** $300,000
- **Other Expenses:** $200,000

## Monitoring and Evaluation

The monitoring and evaluation plan outlines the methods for tracking progress and assessing the impact of the project. It includes indicators for success and a timeline for reporting.

### Indicators

1. **Indicator 1:** Reduction in greenhouse gas emissions.
   - Target: 20% reduction by 2030.
2. **Indicator 2:** Increase in renewable energy use.
   - Target: 50% increase by 2030.
3. **Indicator 3:** Improvement in energy efficiency.
   - Target: 30% improvement by 2030.

## Conclusion

The conclusion summarizes the key points of the proposal and emphasizes the importance of funding to support the project's objectives.

# Green Climate Fund Funding Proposal v.2 | PAGE 8 OF 13

## Assessments of climate change impacts and vulnerabilities

### 1.0
Global, local, urban, rural, and regional flood hazard and technical capacity is extremely constrained, particularly when considering the impact of climate change on infrastructure and communities. The Green Climate Fund (GCF) has been designed to enable countries to access funding for adaptation projects that will help them build resilience to climate impacts. The GCF has been designed to support developing countries in addressing the adverse effects of climate change by providing financial resources to mitigate greenhouse gas emissions and adapt to climate change.

### 2.0
The United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change (UNFCCC) is a landmark international treaty that aims to stabilize greenhouse gas concentrations in the atmosphere at a level that would prevent dangerous anthropogenic interference with the climate system. The UNFCCC has been designed to support developing countries in addressing the adverse effects of climate change by providing financial resources to mitigate greenhouse gas emissions and adapt to climate change.

### 3.0
The United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change (UNFCCC) is a landmark international treaty that aims to stabilize greenhouse gas concentrations in the atmosphere at a level that would prevent dangerous anthropogenic interference with the climate system. The UNFCCC has been designed to support developing countries in addressing the adverse effects of climate change by providing financial resources to mitigate greenhouse gas emissions and adapt to climate change.

## Existing State and Federal Funding Sources for Climate Risk Management and Climate Change Adaptation

### Paragraph - Objective 4.5: Strengthen local water security in Phoenix, Objective 6.2: Enhance water security of the Verde River Basin, and Objective 8.3: Enhance water security of the Salt River Basin.

- **Objective 4.5**: "Strengthen local water security in Phoenix." This objective focuses on improving water management practices and infrastructure to ensure reliable water supplies for urban areas.
- **Objective 6.2**: "Enhance water security of the Verde River Basin." This involves implementing strategies to protect and preserve the water resources within this basin, ensuring sustainable use by communities and ecosystems.
- **Objective 8.3**: "Enhance water security of the Salt River Basin." Similar to Objective 6.2, this focuses on improving water management practices in the Salt River Basin to ensure long-term water security.

### Existing State and Federal Funding Sources

#### Arizona Department of Water Resources (ADWR)

- **Objective 4.5**: ADWR is responsible for implementing strategies that enhance local water security in Phoenix.
- **Objective 6.2**: ADWR works on projects aimed at enhancing the water security of the Verde River Basin.

### Existing DOT Country Programs

#### The DOT Country Programmes also specifically identifies the need for flood and water security programs.

- **Objective 4.5**: DOT Country Programmes focus on improving local water security in Phoenix.
- **Objective 6.2**: These programmes aim to enhance water security in the Verde River Basin.

### Alignment with existing policies such as NDCs, NAPAs, and MAPs

#### The alignment of these objectives with existing policies is crucial for effective implementation.

- **Objective 4.5**: This objective aligns with national policies aimed at enhancing local water security.
- **Objective 6.2**: Policies such as NDCs (Nationally Determined Contributions) and MAPs (Monitoring, Assessment, and Planning) support this objective by providing a framework for action.

### Capacity of Accredited Entities or Executing Entities to deliver

#### The capacity of these entities is critical in ensuring the successful implementation of these objectives.

- **Objective 4.5**: Entities involved must have the technical expertise to implement water security projects.
- **Objective 6.2**: These entities need to be well-equipped with resources and knowledge to enhance water security in the Verde River Basin.

## Role of National Designated Authority and Entities with Non-Governmental Organizations and Other Relevant Stakeholders

1. The addition of the NDA to the list of eligible entities under the GCF, as has been done for other countries such as SPF and the NBA, can be considered. This will allow the NDA to work directly with the private sector and civil society organizations (CSOs) in the implementation of projects. This will also enable the NDA to work closely with the government's COVID-19 response efforts and ensure that these are aligned with the country's long-term development goals.

2. The NDA, working with the government and its focal points, can establish a national coordinating mechanism (NCM) to facilitate the implementation of projects. This NCM will be responsible for the coordination of all projects funded by Pauli, as well as those funded by other entities such as SPF, the NBA, and the GCF.

3. The NDA should also work closely with the government's COVID-19 response efforts to ensure that these are aligned with the country's long-term development goals.

## D

### 18a
The substrate will focus on leveraging best available technology and approaches for the DRR water security projects. The substrate will also focus on developing and implementing innovative solutions to address climate change adaptation needs and ecosystems services, MRV, finance and business development, and other as defined within tasks a through g of this section. **Additional Information:**

- **AS I:** The project aims to reduce greenhouse gas emissions by 20% within the next five years.
- **AS II:** Focuses on expanding renewable energy sources such as solar and wind power.
- **AS III:** Aims to increase agricultural productivity while reducing carbon footprint.
- **AS IV:** Develops urban green spaces to mitigate heat island effects.
- **AS V:** Enhances infrastructure resilience against climate-related disasters.
- **AS VI:** Provides clean energy access to remote communities.
- **AS VII:** Restores degraded ecosystems, enhancing biodiversity and carbon sequestration.
- **AS VIII:** Promotes sustainable farming practices to adapt to changing climatic conditions.

---

1. UNDP and FEMA (2016) Typology Report, Joint Disaster Risk Management Survey - Framework States of Micronesia (FSM)

## Objective

The objective is to provide a standardized framework for the GCF project. This will ensure consistency and comparability across different projects.

### Co-financing Options

- Government Grants
- Private Sector Partnerships
- International Development Agencies
- Carbon Credits
- Non-Governmental Organizations
- Crowdfunding

## Implementation Strategy

The implementation strategy includes the following steps:

1. **Identify Potential Partners**: Engage with government agencies, private sector entities, and international organizations.
2. **Develop Partnership Agreements**: Negotiate terms and conditions for co-financing.
3. **Monitor Progress**: Regularly update stakeholders on project milestones.

### Monitoring and Reporting

- Number of countries benefiting from the GCF project
- Total estimated cost (USD)
- Total GCF funding requested (USD)

## Budget Allocation

The budget allocation is as follows:

- Adaptation to Climate Change: 270,000 USD
- Mitigation of Greenhouse Gases: 450,000 USD
- Enhancement of Climate Resilience: 680,000 USD

## Risk Management

Risk management strategies include:

1. **Financial Risks**: Diversify funding sources to minimize dependency on a single partner.
2. **Operational Risks**: Implement robust project management practices.

### Risk Mitigation Measures

- Regular financial audits
- Comprehensive risk assessments
- Contingency planning for unexpected events

## Conclusion

The standardized framework and co-financing options will enhance the efficiency and effectiveness of GCF projects, ensuring sustainable development outcomes.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL v.2 | PAGE 8 OF 13

## Objective of GCF to meet the needs of developing countries

### 4.1 Objective and scope of the proposal

- **Objective:** To support the implementation of climate change mitigation and adaptation projects in developing countries.
- **Scope:** The proposal covers a range of activities, including renewable energy, energy efficiency, and climate resilience measures.

## Programme and project activities

### 4.2.1 Mitigation of greenhouse gas emissions

- **Activities:**
  - Renewable energy projects (solar, wind)
  - Energy efficiency improvements
  - Sustainable transport initiatives
  - Forest conservation and afforestation

### 4.2.2 Adaptation to climate change impacts

- **Activities:**
  - Early warning systems for extreme weather events
  - Climate-resilient infrastructure development
  - Water management strategies
  - Ecosystem restoration and protection

## Programme implementation arrangements

### 5.1 Training of staff

- **Objective:** To ensure that all project staff are adequately trained to implement the programme effectively.
- **Activities:**
  - Conducting training sessions on climate change mitigation and adaptation strategies
  - Providing technical assistance to local communities
  - Facilitating knowledge-sharing workshops

### 5.2 Gender equality and social inclusion

- **Objective:** To ensure that gender equality and social inclusion are integrated into all programme activities.
- **Activities:**
  - Conducting gender assessments to identify needs and gaps
  - Developing gender-responsive project designs
  - Implementing social inclusion strategies for vulnerable groups

### 5.3 Monitoring, reporting, and verification (MRV)

- **Objective:** To establish a robust MRV system to track progress and ensure accountability.
- **Activities:**
  - Setting up monitoring frameworks and indicators
  - Regular data collection and analysis
  - Publishing annual reports on programme performance

## Disclosure of funded project information

### 6.1 Training of staff

- **Objective:** To enhance the capacity of local communities to implement climate change projects.
- **Activities:**
  - Conducting training sessions for community leaders
  - Providing technical assistance and resources
  - Facilitating knowledge-sharing workshops

### 6.2 Gender equality and social inclusion

- **Objective:** To ensure that gender equality and social inclusion are integrated into all programme activities.
- **Activities:**
  - Conducting gender assessments to identify needs and gaps
  - Developing gender-responsive project designs
  - Implementing social inclusion strategies for vulnerable groups

### 6.3 Enhancement of institutional and legal frameworks

- **Objective:** To strengthen the institutional and legal frameworks for climate change mitigation and adaptation.
- **Activities:**
  - Conducting policy reviews and assessments
  - Providing technical assistance to governments
  - Facilitating regional cooperation on climate change issues
