# Pacific Community

## Compendium of Indicators for Monitoring and Evaluating Gender Equality in Fisheries and Aquaculture

### Category 1.1.1
**Context:** Communities respect local and national laws that protect women's rights to participate in decision-making processes related to fisheries resources.

- **Indicator:** Number of out-groups (e.g., ethnic minorities, indigenous peoples) represented in decision-making bodies at the national level.
- **Data Source:** National statistics on gender equality indicators.

### Category 2.1.2
**Context:** Women's groups advocate for their rights and participate in policy-making processes related to fisheries resources.

- **Indicator:** Number of women's organizations actively advocating for gender equality in fisheries policies.
- **Data Source:** National statistics on gender equality indicators.

### Category 3.1.1
**Context:** Governments recognize the importance of gender equality in fisheries governance.

- **Indicator:** Percentage of national budgets allocated to gender equality programs in fisheries.
- **Data Source:** National budget reports.

### Category 2.2
**Context:** Gender-responsive policies and practices are implemented at all levels of government, including local governments.

- **Indicator:** Number of gender-responsive policies adopted by local governments.
- **Data Source:** Local government policy documents.

### Category 3.2
**Context:** Governments promote gender equality in fisheries governance through targeted programs and initiatives.

- **Indicator:** Percentage of national budgets allocated to gender equality programs in fisheries.
- **Data Source:** National budget reports.

## Totals

| Category | Indicator | Data Source |
|----------|------------|-------------|
| 1.1.1    | Number of out-groups represented in decision-making bodies at the national level | National statistics on gender equality indicators |
| 2.1.2    | Number of women's organizations actively advocating for gender equality in fisheries policies | National statistics on gender equality indicators |
| 3.1.1    | Percentage of national budgets allocated to gender equality programs in fisheries | National budget reports |
| 2.2      | Number of gender-responsive policies adopted by local governments | Local government policy documents |
| 3.2      | Percentage of national budgets allocated to gender equality programs in fisheries | National budget reports |

**Total:** 100,000 (Targeted gender equality indicators implemented)

# Initial Sub-grant Gender Assessment

The sub-grant selection process offers specific to gender will be refined as part of the gender action plan about the sub-grants. The sub-grant gender assessment tool is a guide for the selection committee to assess the gender responsiveness of potential sub-grantees. This guide is based on the gender mainstreaming guidelines published in 2018, below. Given the nature of the sub-grant and broad objectives, this assessment will be used as a general guideline to ensure that sub-grants are being implemented effectively.

Sub-grant that can include, but not limited to specific gender needs of the sub-grant will require having sub-grant implementation plans that address gender equality. The selection committee will use this tool to assess the gender responsiveness of potential sub-grantees. Ultimately, the final decision on whether or not to approve a sub-grant application is at the discretion of the selection committee and SPC will ensure that they are aligned with the GSP Gender Policy Requirements.

## Questioning Gender

| #  | Questions                                                                                                                                                       |
|----|-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| 1  | What key areas does your organization work in?                                                                                                                 |
| 2  | Does your organization have a gender policy or strategy? If yes, please describe.                                                                                 |
| 3  | How do you ensure that women and girls are included in your programs?                                                                                           |
| 4  | What steps has your organization taken to address gender equality issues?                                                                                         |
| 5  | Please provide any other information that demonstrates your commitment to gender equality.                                                                         |
| 6  | What specific actions will you take to ensure the sub-grant is implemented in a gender-responsive manner?                                                          |
| 7  | How specifically will the sub-grant address gender equality issues?                                                                                            |
| 8  | Are there any sub-grant outcomes/results that are specific indicators of gender equality?                                                                         |

# Annex I - Women's Group Consultations Summary

## Consultation with Palung Women's Council

### December 04, 2020: Meeting with Palung Women's Council Intern President, Ms. Maria Devra

The meeting started off with a brief introduction followed by an overview of Pacific Community (SPC) Gender Equality and Social Inclusion (GESI) program changes to support actions for local authorities in the Federated States of Micronesia.

The overall GESI programs was discussed as well as key points from the draft GAP.

Key takeaways from the discussion included:

1. Women's participation is often limited by cultural norms, which can be addressed through awareness and education.
2. Promoting women's leadership and encouraging their involvement in decision-making processes at all levels can help address gender inequality.
3. Although there are different roles for males and females at the household level, both make significant contributions to the family unit.
4. Women have limited access to resources such as land and property due to cultural norms that restrict their ownership of these assets.
5. Women face challenges in accessing services such as health care and education.

### December 04, 2020: Meeting with women group representatives from Kosrae, Yap and Chuuk

#### Kosrae

Participants in the meeting were the YAP Gender Equality Officer representing YAP Women's Council, the President of the Kosrae Women's Association, and the Kosrae Women's Council representing the Chuuk women's group.

The meeting started with a brief introduction and a brief overview of the SPC's Gender Equality and Social Inclusion (GESI) program. The main objective was to discuss the progress of the project results and outcomes for the local authorities in the Federated States of Micronesia.

Participants from Kosrae included the following:

1. Promoting women's participation in leadership positions.
2. Encouraging women's involvement in decision-making processes at all levels.

5. Improving access to services such as health care, education, and economic opportunities.

December 27, 2020: Meeting with Puthpel Women's Council newly elected officials and several of its members.

As at the Puthpel Women's Council meeting presented, Ms. Maria Durm said that the meeting was held with a final recommendation and resulted in the background of the GPC Great Council Fuel GOF (parent Circle) and the background of the GPC Great Council Fuel GOF (parent Circle). The meeting was held to discuss the issues raised by the members of the council, including the need for projects, components, explanations in detail from each of the components.

## Concerns on the objectives

1. Where would the Puthpel Women's Council and its members fit in all these since the primary goal is to support the government?

   The national committee advised this by explaining that although the executing body will be committed to supporting the government, it will also be committed to supporting the needs of the people. This includes the need for women to be represented. Puthpel Women's Council may not directly receive grants under the GPC Great Council Fuel GOF (parent Circle), but they can still benefit from this support by working with other organizations that have direct funding available. They also advised that women's groups can benefit directly from this support.

2. Training on how to implement these objectives?

   The national committee said that all members of these organizations will be trained in the implementation of these projects. This is an important part of the process and will ensure that the projects are implemented effectively. The training will include topics such as planning, budgeting, monitoring, and evaluation. The national committee also advised that women's groups can benefit from this support by working with other organizations that have direct funding available.

3. How would these objectives be achieved?

   The group mentioned that while it is considered that the included in the Order Action Plan is important, it is not the only way to achieve the objectives of the government. They also said that they are committed to supporting the government in achieving its objectives and will continue to work with the people to ensure that these objectives are achieved.

# Pacific Community

The group mentioned that sometimes what is on paper is not what is actually happening on the ground as they are aware of the challenges and difficulties faced by communities where both male and female leaders share the same activities, responsibilities and the overall household in the project making things.

When asked what they would consider as priority small change adaptation projects in relation to the 3 areas mentioned:

1. Water security projects that include water tanks and all its associated equipment to deliver water to individual households. Clean burning stoves at all households.
2. Support for women's groups.
3. Support for youth groups.

There is no consensus on priority projects, but they agreed that their communities could benefit from food and water security projects. They felt that these groups are often left out of the planning process and that including them in the planning would be a good idea to be included in all of the projects and rapidly building.

There is a consensus with the group that they would like to be involved in every aspect of the project and that they would like to see more women's groups involved. They also present an opportunity and not a hindrance from participating in projects that promote community
